#7458. Games Recruiter - China Market (中国招聘专员) at Yodo1

🔥 Forbes’ Top 30 Companies for Remote Jobs 🔥

You will: Connect exceptional talent across China's gaming industry with a global team through proactive, locally informed, human-centric recruiting.

Must have: Hands-on recruitment experience in China, proven sourcing skills across BOSS, Maimai, Liepin and more, strong knowledge of China's gaming talent market, and fluent Mandarin with working English.

At Yodo1, we don’t just grow games—we help turn them into household names.

With billions of downloads across our portfolio, we’ve spent more than a decade shaping the growth of iconic titles. From helping first-time creators monetize their debut games, to bringing world-class IPs from Hasbro, Paramount, Legendary, and Sony Pictures into the hands of top studios such as Rivergame, Candivore, 37Games, and Tencent Studios—we’ve proven what’s possible when creativity meets global expertise.

Yodo1 Games is raising the bar: transforming great titles into lasting franchises that define gaming for generations. Drawing on experience with successes like Transformers: Earth Wars, Rodeo Stampede, and Ski Safari, we provide the systems, partnerships, and global reach that help visionary developers build worlds players will return to for decades.

Joining Yodo1 Games means more than a job—it means becoming part of a team that lives and breathes games, works with some of the most talented creators in the industry, and is driven by a mission to shape the future of gaming.

We love games. We play games. And together, we grow games that last.

We believe in the future and power of the mobile gaming industry and that everyone deserves an equal opportunity to build a life they love through games—from anywhere in the world.

We believe you know when and where you do your best work. We are fully remote by design and we WFA: Work From Anywhere. We don’t impose hours or limit time off.

We are driven by performance and we experiment often. We care about impact, not titles.

We are looking for a hands-on Games Recruiter who knows where to find great talent in China's gaming industry and how to engage them. You will own recruitment for China-market roles, bring local market insight to our global recruitment team, and build strong pipelines for specialist and hard-to-fill positions.

Focused on impact. You understand the 80/20 rule and focus on activities that matter.

Performance-driven and growth-oriented. You have an intrinsic will to succeed while unafraid to try, fail, and learn by experimenting with new approaches and testing ideas.

Cooperative. You help others succeed; you give and receive feedback well.

Humble. You ask for help; you are quick to admit and learn from your mistakes.

SMART. You work and communicate in ways that are Specific, Measurable, Actionable, Relevant, and Time-bound.

Own full-cycle recruitment for China market positions (and global when needed) and closing hires for roles in sales, marketing, game production, and beyond.

Partner with the recruitment team, hiring managers and hiring teams to calibrate role requirements and business needs

Proactively research, source and engage top talent using Chian hiring platforms i.e. BOSS, Maimai, Liepin and more, including WeChat networks and other effective local channels.

Build and maintain talent maps and pipelines across China's gaming ecosystem

Conduct pre-screen interviews (in Mandarin and English) that assess technical skills as well as culture add. Provide thoughtful feedback and ensure that the candidates you present align with our values and needs.

Represent Yodo1’s brand as an ambassador during every stage of the hiring process. Make sure candidates understand what it means to work here and what makes us unique.

Work closely with hiring managers to develop accurate job descriptions, share insights on market trends, and ensure business and recruiting alignment.

Attend local Chinese games related networking conferences.

Stay up to date on the latest recruitment trends, tools, and best practices.

Experiment with AI and new sourcing approaches to improve our hiring quality and efficiency.

Proven experience managing the full recruitment cycle, from sourcing to closing, ideally in a fast-paced environment with a demonstrated ability to hire across various roles.

Strong knowledge of the Chinese gaming market (or adjacent technology) talent ecosystem

Ability to confidently use BOSS, Maimai, Liepin and more to identify and engage candidates; familiarity with additional China market channels and networks.

Fluent Mandarin Chinese and professional working English, with the ability to communicate clearly with candidates and international colleagues.

Strong EQ and communication skills; ability to connect with candidates and provide a positive and engaging experience while challenging them with insightful questions.

The ability to build and maintain strong stakeholder relationships, acting as a strategic partner to hiring managers and interviewers.

A data-driven approach; comfortable using data and tools to track hiring progress, measure your outcomes, and refine strategies for better results.

A creative approach to using AI to improve candidate experience and effectively manage large applicant pools.

The ability to prioritize effectively and keep the hiring process on track, even when managing multiple roles.

Experience recruiting across mobile games, LiveOps, game publishing, monetization or gaming business development.

Experience hiring internationally, outside the China market.

Experience working in a fully remote, international business coordinating with colleagues in multiple time zones.

Familiarity with Teamtailor or a similar ATS, and practical use of AI to support recruitment workflows.

Compensation. We pay based on qualifications, experience, and fit for the role. We provide an allowance to purchase your own device or equipment and offer variable compensation through our annual bonus program.

Growth. We offer an environment where you will have the trust and freedom to try, fail, learn fast, and make a real impact while accelerating your career, and where anyone with a great idea can pitch it directly to our management team.

WFA. Work from anywhere in the world, travel when and where you like, get paid in your currency of choice, and enjoy flexible hours and unlimited paid time off.

Community. Work with an energetic, fast-learning, supportive, and diverse team of Yodies across six continents and 30+ countries who share and support new ideas and get things done.

Ready to change the game for mobile game developers and build your career from anywhere?

New remote jobs delivered to your inbox, every week.

Location independence is the future of work. It serves companies with best talent from around the globe. And benefits employees with a greater work life balance and the ability to work in their pyjamas.- Nithur, founder.

#7457. 21 Alternative Search Engines You Can Use Instead of Google via @sejournal, @lorenbaker

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Where search leaders are putting 2027 budget, plus the AI visibility scorecard that replaces session reporting.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

For many people, searching the web means opening Google. It’s a familiar place to research a purchase, check a fact, or catch up on the news and that reach shows in the numbers with Google accounting for 91.04% of worldwide search engine referrals according to Statcounter in October 2026.

But the search engine you use out of habit is not always the best tool for the job. Think about the last time you needed an image you could reuse, a webpage that had disappeared, or a more private way to search. Those are different problems, and a single search box is not always the easiest way to solve them.

You don’t have to give up Google to find out what you might be missing. We collated 21 alternative search engines and tools worth trying, whether you’re looking for a better answer or a new way for customers to find your business.

About the Usage Figures: Entries are ordered within each section by estimated monthly visits to the named website or subdomain. The October 2026 figures are not unique users, search queries, or app activity. Statcounter percentages shown separately measure search referral share, not visits.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 6 billion to chatgpt.com, October 2026. This covers the ChatGPT website, not Search alone.

ChatGPT is useful when your question needs a conversation rather than a fresh set of keywords each time. You can ask it to compare two approaches, explain an unfamiliar term, and then look for recent evidence without starting over. Its advantage is keeping the context of that investigation together.

OpenAI’s search documentation says it can retrieve current web information and link to sources. Search is available on free and paid plans, including for people who are not signed in, although usage limits apply. ChatGPT may search automatically, or you can select Search from its tools.

Use it to work through a question, then open the citations to check the answer. It can misread a source or miss a qualification, and its use of other search providers means it is not simply a separate, independent web index.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 88.3 million to perplexity.ai, October 2026.

Perplexity puts source-backed answers at the center of the search experience. Its research-oriented approach is useful when you want an overview of a subject and a trail of sources to investigate, rather than opening a long list of results before finding the main points.

Its Pro Search process can conduct multiple searches and bring findings together, with follow-up questions keeping the research in context. Depending on your plan, you can narrow the work to areas such as academic or financial sources. Access and limits vary by tier.

The benefit is a faster route into a topic, not a substitute for reading the evidence. Check that each citation supports the statement beside it because AI answers can still contain hallucinations even when links are present.

Why SEO Experts Should Care: Perplexity can be useful even if it sends less traffic than Google. In his post on why marketers should care about Perplexity, Dennis Goedegebuure argues that it is a low-cost test bench for AI search. Try the research questions your customers ask, inspect which pages get cited, and check how your brand and recently updated facts appear. For B2B marketers, that means testing visibility during product research, rather than judging the opportunity solely by overall visit totals.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 3.7 billion to bing.com, October 2026.

Bing is a full-service alternative for everyday web, image, video, and news searches. One concrete reason to try it is Visual Search: when you have a picture of a product, plant, or object but do not know its name, you can start with the image instead of guessing keywords.

Microsoft’s visual search tools can find related images and shopping options, identify objects, and extract or translate text from a picture. Conventional results remain available alongside AI-assisted tools, so an answer does not have to be the end of your search.

Search Engine Journal covered Bing’s search updates in December 2024, including work on faster, more precise results. Try the same image or video query on Bing and Google to see which makes the next step easier for you; that is more useful than assuming one wins every search.

Where available, Bing’s Copilot Search adds AI-generated summaries with source citations and suggested follow-up topics, so you can explore a question without starting from scratch.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 751 million to search.yahoo.com, October 2026.

Search Engine Referral Share: 2.54% in the U.S., October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Yahoo Search makes the most sense if you already use Yahoo for news, finance, or other daily information. Its value is the connection between search and that wider content ecosystem, rather than a completely different index of the web.

Yahoo introduced Scout in January 2026 as an AI answer engine combining open-web information with Yahoo content and data. The U.S. beta launch named Anthropic’s Claude and Microsoft’s Bing grounding API as parts of the system.

That gives Yahoo users a way to move from a headline or financial topic into a sourced explanation. You can still browse traditional search results, and you should follow Scout’s source links when an answer matters. The launch’s audience figures cover Yahoo’s broader services, not people using Scout alone.

At Search Engine Journal, Yahoo accounts for about 0.2% of our traffic. It’s a small share, but it still brings a few thousand visits in a month and for a different business, even that smaller stream of visitors could include a potential customer or lead to a sale.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 1.7 billion to baidu.com, October 2026. This includes non-search activity on the named website.

Search Engine Referral Share: 49% in China, October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Baidu matters when your research or marketing is aimed at Chinese-language audiences. Its value is not simply in translating an English query, but it offers a view of Chinese-language search and a connected set of local content services.

Alongside web results, its homepage links to maps, images, video, the Baike encyclopedia, Zhidao questions and answers, and Wenku documents. Those services give you distinct places to investigate how a topic, product, or brand is described. Use the terminology that local sources use rather than assuming an English search will surface the same material.

For marketers, the search referral share is a reason to investigate Baidu separately, not proof that it will send a particular site traffic. Distinguish content hosted inside Baidu’s services from results linking to external publishers, and assess the original source.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 1.5 billion to naver.com, October 2026. This includes non-search activity on the named website.

Search Engine Referral Share: 42.25% in South Korea, October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Naver is useful for Korean-language research because its search experience brings together more than external webpages. Naver’s search overview describes results across news, maps, cafés, blogs, images, video, and other categories.

That mix gives you a route into local experiences and community content as well as conventional reporting. Its community services include Naver Blog, interest-based Café communities, and Knowledge iN questions and answers. If you are researching a Korean product or audience, those are different sources to explore from an English-language web-results page.

For website owners, Naver Search Advisor is the official starting point for webmaster tools. Keep visits to an external website separate from engagement with a blog or other content hosted inside Naver.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 162.2 million to yandex.com, October 2026. This covers the international website, not total use across all Yandex domains.

Search Engine Referral Share: 70.22% in Russia, October 2026. This measures referrals in its sample, not people or total queries.

Yandex is worth checking when you need Russian-language sources or are researching visibility in Russia. Its large share of measured referrals there gives it a different role from a small global alternative – it is an important regional search environment in its own right.

Use it to compare which local publishers and pages appear for the terminology your audience actually uses. Its international homepage also presents an AI search option, but the practical reason to investigate it is the regional results and audience context, not simply another chatbot interface.

SEJ’s Yandex guide provides background for marketers. The 2023 data leak is a separate historical event, not a verified description of today’s ranking system. The search referral share above describes referrals, not the percentage of Russian people using Yandex.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 16.5 million to sogou.com, October 2026.

Sogou offers another route into Chinese-language information, with a more specific reason to try it rather than simply comparing general web results – its WeChat search is a destination for discovering public accounts and articles from that ecosystem.

That is useful when the material you need was published as a WeChat article rather than an ordinary standalone webpage. Search in the relevant Chinese terminology and distinguish those public articles from results in Sogou’s general web search.

This is not access to private messages or every piece of content in WeChat. As with other platform-specific searches, check the account, publication date, and original article before using a result as evidence.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 698.8 million to search.brave.com, October 2026.

Brave Search combines an independent web index with tools for changing how results are ranked. It is worth trying if you want both an alternative to Google or Bing’s underlying results and more say over which sources rise to the top.

Its search help describes Goggles, which apply alternative ranking or filtering rules. Brave also describes domain-level ranking controls and an AI answer experience on its about page. Those controls give you something more specific to experiment with than a different-looking results page.

SEJ reported Brave’s Bing separation in 2023. You can use Brave Search in another browser, so testing its index does not require adopting Brave’s browser, wallet, VPN, or other products.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 681.9 million to duckduckgo.com, October 2026.

DuckDuckGo is a straightforward choice if you want everyday search without a personal search-history profile. You can use its search website in your existing browser, so the switch does not require changing your whole browsing setup.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 57.6 million to startpage.com, October 2026.

Its partner explanation identifies both Google and Microsoft Bing. Startpage says it does not share personal information with them, and that those providers see Startpage rather than the person making the request.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 4.6 million to kagi.com, October 2026.

Its Lenses let you narrow searches to selected websites or categories such as forums, academic sources, or PDFs. Kagi’s source documentation describes its own indexes alongside major and specialist providers, with an emphasis on surfacing independent and noncommercial content. It is not powered solely by its Teclis index.

As of November 2026, its monthly individual plans include Starter at $5 plus tax for 300 searches and Professional at $10 plus tax for unlimited searches. Annual billing has different terms. If you search heavily and want persistent control over sources, those features are the reason to consider paying.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 219 million to ecosia.org, October 2026.

Ecosia lets you put the revenue from an everyday habit toward climate work. It says all of its profits go to climate action, with the majority funding tree-planting projects. That is its central reason to exist: changing who benefits financially from search.

The company publishes monthly financial reports, so you can look beyond the tree counter to see how money is spent. Its provider documentation lists Bing, Google, and European Search Perspective, or EUSP; the mix depends on factors including region, device, and permissions.

This makes Ecosia a funding-model choice, not a promise that every query uses a wholly separate index. If you want to support its mission while keeping familiar web search, it is a low-friction option to try. You can check the provider through the information control beside a result.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 451,800 to karmasearch.org, October 2026.

KARMA applies the search-funded nonprofit model to animal welfare and biodiversity. Its distinction from a broader climate-focused service is the cause: helping fund organizations working on wildlife, habitats, and animal protection.

The company’s explanation says sponsored search links generate revenue for its nonprofit partners. Its site lists partners including Re:wild, Humane World for Animals, and iNaturalist. You can inspect those partnerships to decide whether they match the work you want to support.

Availability varies by country. During this review, a U.S. capture showed the search homepage, while a visit from another region displayed a notice that KARMA was unavailable there and would redirect searches to Bing. Check local availability before making it your default; a regional restriction does not mean the service has closed.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 155,800 to yep.com, October 2026.

Yep is an Ahrefs-built alternative with a distinctive audience beyond people typing into a search box: developers who want web results for an AI agent or research application. Ahrefs’ current Yep product page describes a first-party crawler, index, and ranking system, with result excerpts and filters for the information an application retrieves.

The public search interface captured for this review also returned conventional web results. For an everyday user, that makes it another index to compare; for a developer, the API is the clearer reason to investigate it. The current homepage emphasizes that developer-facing use rather than presenting a Google-like consumer pitch.

SEJ’s 2022 Yep interview explains the original proposal to share advertising revenue with creators. Although, the current homepage does not establish that those proposed payouts are operating, so they should not be the reason you choose it today.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 4.4 billion to x.com, October 2026. This covers platform website activity, not searches or app usage.

x.com is useful when the thing you need is a public statement or conversation: what an organization announced, what a named person said, or how a developing event is being discussed. You are searching posts on the platform, not a general index of webpages.

That direct access to posted statements is its advantage for news monitoring and research. Its advanced search is a further option for signed-in users; the public Explore route redirected to a sign-in screen during this review.

Use posts to locate leads and original statements, not as automatic confirmation of a claim. Check the account, timestamp, and supporting evidence, especially when an old post is circulating as if it describes a new event.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 2.1 billion to linkedin.com, October 2026. This covers platform website activity, not searches or app usage.

LinkedIn is useful when your search starts with a professional identity, such as a person, employer, role, or job. It can also help you find practitioners discussing a work-related subject, rather than only the publishers ranking for that subject on the open web.

The advantage is the professional context. A name alongside an employer and career history gives you a way to distinguish people who share a name and identify a relevant source. Its public people search is one starting point, although fuller profiles and other features can require signing in.

Treat profiles as self-reported information, not a verified company directory. Check current roles and dates against the person’s or organization’s own website when those details matter to your research.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 46.4 million to web.archive.org, October 2026.

The Internet Archive’s Wayback Machine answers a question a current search result often cannot: What did this webpage say before it changed or disappeared? It is useful for checking an old product claim, recovering a removed reference, or following the history of a website.

Enter the URL and inspect the available captures. The advantage is the dated record where you can compare versions rather than relying on a current page to explain its own past. Check the capture date and whether images and other resources were preserved.

The Wayback Machine is part of the wider Internet Archive, whose books, recordings, software, and other collections are separate resources. It is not a complete archive of every page, and a captured claim is evidence of what a page said then, not proof that the claim was true.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 39.3 million to slideshare.net, October 2026.

SlideShare is worth trying when you want the presentation itself, not another article summarizing the subject. A conference talk, research deck, or business pitch can reveal how someone structures an argument and illustrates the details.

The current homepage describes a collection of more than 25 million presentations. Browsing, reading, and uploading do not require a subscription, while features such as downloading presentations are paid benefits. The value is a format-specific collection you can explore for research or presentation inspiration.

Check the uploader and publication date before relying on a deck’s statistics or recommendations. Being able to view a presentation also does not automatically give you permission to reuse its artwork.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 3.1 million to wolframalpha.com, October 2026.

WolframAlpha is for questions that need computation rather than a list of pages. If you want to solve an equation, convert units, or work with structured facts, it can calculate an answer from its data and algorithms instead of leaving you to find a suitable calculator elsewhere.

Its explanation of the service describes dynamic computation over curated knowledge. The mathematics examples show the inputs it understands; other areas include science, dates, and measures.

That is a different job from comparing recent reporting or finding an opinion. Use it when the result should be computed, and check how it interpreted your units, assumptions, and wording before relying on the output.

Estimated Monthly Web Visits: 296,200 to openverse.org, October 2026.

Openverse is the tool to try when finding a usable image or audio track matters more than finding a webpage about one. It focuses on openly licensed and public-domain media, making it useful for an article, presentation, or other creative project.

Its About page describes a collection of more than 800 million images and audio tracks gathered from multiple repositories, with features such as one-click attribution. That collection size is separate from its website traffic. Its advantage is bringing reuse-oriented discovery into one place, rather than treating every image on the web as equally available to use.

Openly licensed does not mean free of conditions. Licenses can require attribution or restrict commercial use, and Openverse does not verify every work’s licensing information. Follow through to the original source and confirm the license before using a file.

The introduction of ChatGPT helped bring conversational interfaces into everyday use. In January 2023, SEJ reported that Google’s founders had responded by returning to discussions about its AI response and then opened access to Bard in March 2023.

Google rolled out AI Overviews to U.S. users in May 2024 and announced AI Mode in 2025. Those developments explain why many services in this list now combine web results with AI generated answers. The two formats can help with different parts of the same research task.

Google may be the most familiar option, but the best choice for a particular task can be different. Try a few real queries on two or three services, compare the sources, the ease of finding the original information, and the controls you will actually use.

You can keep one engine for everyday searches and use another for a specific job. A calculation, an archived webpage, and a professional profile do not need to start in the same search box.

Open your browser’s search settings and choose a supported default engine. If the service is not listed, use its official browser setup instructions. You can also visit its website directly without changing your default.

Some do. Startpage identifies Google and Bing as partners. DuckDuckGo largely sources traditional links and images from Bing. Kagi combines its own indexes with several providers. Brave Search builds an independent web index.

No. DuckDuckGo says it is an independent company. A search provider relationship is different from ownership, and its traditional links and images largely come from Bing.

No search tool can guarantee a neutral view of everything online. Index coverage, ranking rules, filters, location, and the query itself affect what appears. Compare independent sources and more than one search engine when the differences matter.

#7456. Snapchat adds more creator partnership opportunities

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The platform is testing an updated version of its Snap Creator Network, as well as new Visited By listings on its Snap Map.

Snapchat announced several new creator discovery and monetization opportunities, including an updated creator marketplace, new location markers for where creators have visited on the Snap Map, and an advanced agency partner program.

First, Snapchat is testing a refreshed version of its Snap Creator Network, which aims to make it easier for brands to find relevant creators to work with on Snapchat ad campaigns.

As shown in this screenshot, Snapchat’s updated creator discovery process uses artificial intelligence to better match brands with relevant creators.

As explained by Snapchat: “Advertisers can describe the exact creator or content they’re looking for, including audience, category, or campaign goals, and the Snap Creator Network will surface the best fit. Then, brands can explore the performance of creators, save them to shortlists, message them to establish partnerships, or even boost organic content, all in one place.”

Snapchat originally announced this creator marketplace upgrade in June, but it’s now launched the platform in open beta testing.

Snapchat has also introduced a new way for physical stores and locations to benefit from creator content, with Visited By listings on the Snap Map.

As shown in these examples, Visited By will add markers to places where creators that the user follows have filmed content. The highlighted listings will also include links to the relevant posts.

This could help brands tap into influencer recommendations, while also providing more incentive for stores to collaborate with popular creators in order to increase their presence in the Snap Map display.

Snapchat recently reported that 450 million people use Snap Map every month.

Finally, Snapchat launched an expanded Creator Agency Partner Program, which will provide agencies with additional training and support to ensure that they’re offering their clients the best guidance on creator marketing campaigns.

As per Snapchat, its updated Creator Agency Partner Program will include two tiers:

That will ensure that more agency providers are aware of the company’s evolving creator partnership efforts, and how to utilize its advanced discovery tools to guide brands towards the right creator partners.

Snapchat said the updated Creator Agency Partner Program is now in operation for agencies in the U.S., U.K. and the Middle East and North Africa.

#7454. Proxify AB: Senior SecOps Engineer

We are looking for anInfrastructure & Security Operations Engineer on behalf of our client to strengthen cyber defense, reduce organizational risk, and drive core security improvements across cloud, identity, endpoint, and server environments.

In this role, you will play a central part in vulnerability remediation, incident response, security monitoring, and infrastructure hardening. You will collaborate closely with infrastructure, cloud, service delivery, and third-party vendors to turn security priorities and audit findings into verified, production-ready fixes.

3+ years of hands-on experience in Security Operations (SecOps), Infrastructure Security, or Systems Administration.

Strong working knowledge of hybrid enterprise environments (Microsoft Entra ID, Active Directory, M365, Azure, or AWS).

Demonstrated experience with vulnerability scanning/management tools and patch coordination across multiple platforms.

Practical experience working with SIEM, EDR/XDR, or managed SOC/MDR escalation workflows.

Proven ability to coordinate technical remediation across internal teams and third-party managed service providers.

Strong analytical troubleshooting skills with a structured approach to documentation and tracking.

Hands-on experience with tools like Microsoft Defender XDR, Sentinel, CrowdStrike, SentinelOne, Qualys, Tenable, or Rapid7.

Experience with scripting and automation (PowerShell, KQL, Bash, or Python).

Familiarity with frameworks such as ISO 27001, Cyber Essentials Plus, CIS Benchmarks, or PCI DSS.

Relevant industry certifications (e.g., CompTIA Security+, CySA+, SC-200, AZ-500, or equivalent).

Monitor, triage, and respond to security alerts from SIEM, EDR/XDR, and MDR/MSSP partners; support containment, recovery, root-cause analysis, and post-incident reviews.

Identify, prioritize, and drive remediation of vulnerabilities across infrastructure, cloud, and endpoint estates in coordination with system owners and external vendors.

Implement baseline configurations and hardening standards across hybrid environments (Microsoft 365, Entra ID, Azure/AWS, Active Directory, networks, and remote access).

Assist with privileged access management (PAM), MFA enforcement, Conditional Access, and periodic access reviews.

Refine detection coverage, operational playbooks, and security procedures; maintain remediation records and assist with compliance audit evidence collection.

Deliver concise status updates on vulnerability posture, open risks, SLAs, and technical blockers to key stakeholders.

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.

Remember every detail and next step, without a meeting bot.

#7453. Meta brings its updated Creator Dashboard to the web

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The new dashboard will replace other management tools as the primary interface for managing a creator’s in-app presence.

Creators and social media managers, get ready for Meta to redirect you toward yet another content management tool, which will be slightly different from the one that you just got used to.

Meta announced on Wednesday that its new Creator Dashboard is now active on the web, and that it will replace other management tools as the primary UI for managing a creator’s in-app presence.

Meta has been rolling out its updated Creator Dashboard on mobile devices over the past year, including its Creator Studio app. These new systems offer more artificial intelligence tools and more video creation options, aligning with broader trends.

The updated functionalities are now available on desktop as well, which should provide expanded capacity for overall management and oversight.

But it will also mean learning the specifics of another new management tool.

This sentence acts as a pretty good summary of the confusion that Meta regularly causes with changes to its content management apps: “Creator Dashboard’s revamp began last year, back when it was still called Professional Dashboard. Since then it has become Creator Dashboard, with Business Dashboard as the counterpart for businesses, so the tools in front of you as a creator are the ones that matter most.”

As per this overview, Meta has several management tools available, which also includes Meta Business Suite and the controls users can access in-stream. Meta keeps updating these systems to ensure that its management tools incorporate the latest functionality, in line with its evolving systems.

But understanding which platform creators and social media managers are supposed to be using can get confusing.

Meta said the updated web composer in Creator Dashboard has been redesigned for feed posts and Reels, with full-screen overviews and live previews, so creators can see what their post will look like before publishing.

There’s also bulk video upload options, an improved content library and more insights into content performance.

Content protection is also available in the new app. Meta's content protection process enables creators to register their original uploads, which Meta’s system can then use as a reference point to detect when others re-post the same thing. Meta expanded content protection to Instagram earlier this week.

Meta said that creators will also now be able to invite people who’ve checked out their content to become followers from the Creator Dashboard, and access Messenger on the web. There’s also a monetization section, where creators will be able to access relevant information about potential monetization opportunities.

So, more Facebook and Instagram management and creation tools, integrated within a single app. For now, at least, till Meta comes out with the next iteration of its management platform and forces everyone to learn an all-new system once again.

#7449. TikTok releases 2026 Music Impact Report

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The analysis found that 94% of songs on the Billboard Global 200 in 2025 had a viral moment on TikTok before climbing the chart.

TikTok has published its 2026 Music Impact Report, which looks at the role that the platform plays in broader music and artist discovery.

The report is based on a study conducted by Luminate, in partnership with TikTok, which incorporates responses from more than 4,000 U.S. TikTok users. The response data underlines just how influential TikTok has become as a music and artist discovery tool, with 45% of U.S. TikTok users saying they discover new music in the app. That, according to the report, is higher than on YouTube, Instagram or Facebook.

The most significant claim of the report, however, is that 94% of songs that entered the Billboard Global 200 in 2025 went viral on TikTok before climbing the chart.

That’s a significant correlation, which is up from 84% 2024, per TikTok’s 2025 Music Impact report.

The data underlines TikTok’s value as a music discovery platform, which makes it a critically important consideration for musicians and publishers when it comes to mapping out their promotional approach.

The data underlines the platform’s growing role as a music discovery tool. In some ways, TikTok has replaced the MTV of old as a key link between music trends and audiences.

That makes it a more valuable consideration for music-related marketers, while the insights also highlight the broader role that the platform plays in informing broader cultural shifts.

Which could be an important consideration for brands seeking to align their campaigns with what’s trending.

#7446. Holiday TV ads from retailers arrive earlier, more frequently in 2026

While retail historically commands the season, it accounted for 63.7% of holiday ads in October, pacing ahead of recent years.

The holidays are always a busy time for advertising, and Nielsen’s data show activity moving further up the calendar as brands try to make the most of a packed Q4. More campaigns are starting as early as September, with holiday creative this year accounting for 1% of all linear TV ads during the month. Even as ad-supported streaming expands, linear remains on a growth trajectory after surpassing $1 billion in spend in 2023, according to Nielsen.

Some elements of the holidays remain constant, including retail’s dominance, which is particularly pronounced so far this season. The category has commanded an average of 45% of linear TV ad spend over the past five years, and only a slightly smaller share of the burgeoning streaming environment. In October, 63.7% of holiday ads came from retailers.

Promotional messaging is a major theme, with over one-third of retail ads assessed by Nielsen over the past five years carrying mentions of Black Friday or Cyber Monday deals events. Retailers are currently under pressure to reach wary shoppers during a persistent run of inflation. Several campaigns have been themed around more thoughtful, personalized gifting instead of low prices alone.

Effective holiday strategies can boost performance, per Nielsen connected TV benchmark studies for 2025. Cosmetics brands recorded a 3.2 times sales lift compared to non-holiday campaigns while department stores experienced a 2.4 times sales lift, the measurement firm found. Restaurants and fast-food brands recorded sales lifts of 1.5 times and 1 times, respectively, versus non-holiday ads.

Outside of retail, category spending trends have fluctuated more noticeably over time. Automotive shifted from representing 16% of holiday linear TV ad spend in 2023 to just 7.7% in 2025. Nielsen attributed the retreat to a bigger focus on streaming, where auto makes up 18.4% of the market. A dearth of car commercials, once a fixture of prime time programming, has gained media attention as the industry navigates economic headwinds and the failure of electric vehicle bets to take off in the U.S.

Food and beverage advertising also dipped between 2024 and 2025, which Nielsen attributed to big brands tightening their budgets and challenger brands only running smaller experiments for the holidays. Large food and beverage marketers including Pepsi and Oreo have rolled out holiday efforts in the past few weeks, with the latter introducing its first new seasonal creative in five years.

#7442. Ad Tech Briefing: Google’s antitrust troubles aren’t over

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This Ad Tech Briefing covers the latest in ad tech and platforms for Digiday+ members and is distributed over email every Tuesday at 10 a.m. ET. More from the series →

Developments last week showed that Google faces another potential antitrust setback, this time in Europe, as it prepares for the next stage of its U.S. ad tech monopoly case.

On Nov. 12, an adviser to the European Union’s highest court recommended overturning a 2024 judgment that annulled an earlier €1.49 billion ($1.7 billion) fine against Google over its AdSense for Search business.

In her ⁠opinion, Advocate General Juliane Kokott argued that the EU’s General Court made legal errors when it overturned the European Commission’s original 2019 penalty. Specifically, Kokott concluded that the lower court had imposed excessive requirements on regulators when assessing whether Google’s contractual restrictions harmed competition.

The case concerns agreements Google used with publishers that displayed search advertisements through its AdSense for Search service; i.e., it involves a different business unit than the one under scrutiny in its most recent U.S. trial.

Per the European proceedings’ plaintiffs, between 2006 and 2016, Google imposed contractual restrictions that limited those websites’ ability to display advertisements from rival search advertising providers.

The European Commission identified three types of restrictions: exclusivity clauses preventing publishers from using competing services, placement clauses restricting where rival advertisements could appear, and provisions requiring Google’s prior approval before changes to advertising displays.

The Commission concluded that these practices constituted a single, continuous infringement of European competition law. Google challenged the decision, and in September 2024, the General Court annulled the fine, finding that regulators had failed to examine relevant circumstances adequately, particularly the duration of Google’s contractual arrangements.

Kokott’s opinion, published Nov. 12, argued that the General Court had placed excessive emphasis on the duration of individual agreements rather than considering their collective effect on competition.

In the accompanying press release, the court explained that regulators could assess contracts collectively to determine whether they could produce exclusionary effects. Kokott recommended setting aside the 2024 judgment and returning the case to the General Court for reconsideration.

Importantly, the recommendation does not automatically reinstate the original fine. The opinion is nonbinding, and the Court of Justice has yet to issue its judgment. Moreover, Kokott acknowledged that the penalty should ultimately be reduced because certain findings concerning Google’s conduct in 2016 are no longer being contested.

Nevertheless, the opinion creates the prospect of another prolonged legal battle over Google’s advertising practices, as the development comes two months after Google avoided a forced breakup of its advertising technology business in the U.S.

In September, federal judge Leonie Brinkema rejected the Justice Department’s proposed divestiture of Google’s AdX exchange and publisher ad server, DoubleClick for Publishers.

Instead, the court accepted most of the government’s proposed behavioral remedies, subject to modifications. The decision shifted the focus from Google’s ownership of its advertising infrastructure to the restrictions governing how that infrastructure operates.

The remedies are intended to address Google’s ability to favor its own advertising products, including through changes affecting interoperability, auction transparency and access for competing ad tech companies. However, the precise obligations and their enforcement remain central questions as the parties work through the final judgment.

Google has indicated that it intends to appeal the underlying liability decision, potentially extending the litigation beyond implementation of the behavioral remedies.

The European and American cases concern different markets and legal questions, but both illustrate the difficulty regulators face in challenging Google’s advertising dominance.

In Europe, the courts are still examining conduct that ended a decade ago. In the U.S., the argument has moved from whether Google violated competition law to whether the remedies will materially change market behavior.

However, even successful antitrust enforcement does not guarantee that publishers and advertisers will move their business away from Google.

Meanwhile, last week’s proceedings demonstrated how The Trade Desk’s struggles highlight a different problem — a development that embodies the scale of the challenges independent competitors to Google, etc., face when challenging Big Tech.

On Nov. 9, The Trade Desk reported a rare quarterly revenue decline, with third-quarter sales falling approximately 0.5% to $736 million from $739 million a year earlier. While the company did beat Wall Street’s (modest) expectations, the result marked a sharp reversal for a company that had long delivered double-digit growth.

The results underscored the difficulties facing the company during what executives have described as a transitional year. The slowdown comes amid growing competition from Amazon, shifting agency relationships and the increasing use of AI to automate media buying.

While The Trade Desk has invested heavily in its Kokai platform and related AI capabilities, those initiatives have yet to restore its historical growth rates, with executives there also revealing how they are reconsidering how acquisitions might contribute to its next phase of growth.

On the company’s earnings call, Jeff Green, The Trade Desk, CEO, revealed leadership were taking a closer look at potential acquisitions, suggesting a more active approach to M&A than the company has historically pursued.

For years, The Trade Desk positioned itself as an independent alternative to the advertising ecosystems operated by Google, Amazon, and other major technology companies, with this pitch a key selling point when articulating its value in the responsible use of CMOs’ media investments.

However, its latest results demonstrate that independence alone is not enough to guarantee growth.

Regulators may eventually make Google’s advertising infrastructure more accessible to competitors. But those competitors must still persuade advertisers that their platforms offer better technology, performance, and economics. The Trade Desk’s difficulties suggest that the industry’s competitive challenges extend well beyond Google’s market power.

Four figures from  ⁠Liftoff Mobile’s Q3 2026 financial results:

#7438. What exactly counts as a creator? The industry still can’t agree

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Every brand seems to have different definitions when it comes to the creator economy. What one brand calls a tastemaker, another labels an influencer. If marketers can’t agree on creator taxonomy, standardization — on everything from measurement to cost — may remain further away than the industry would like.

Since the ballooning of the creator economy, marketers have been trying to standardize everything from what counts as a view, brand-creator contractual language and the framework for deal fee structures. According to the IAB’s latest creator economy report, at least 23% of marketers say the industry would benefit from a unified creator taxonomy.

“That’s the blurry line. Who’s a mega influencer and who’s a celebrity? I don’t know anymore,” said Mindy Murray, director of influencer and content at General Mills.

General Mills’ approach to creator definitions is nuanced. The highest tier is celebrity status, where partnerships often go beyond social and into product design and formula. Mega creators are positioned just below celebrity partnerships. Mid creators range from 500,000 to 1.5 million social media followers. There are super fans or brand advocates who are dedicated creators who are positing consistently on behalf of the brand. Finally, there’s friends and family, which is General Mills’ organic influencer group who also act as brand advocates.

Shutterfly has a three-tier creator codification. Tastemakers are social media stars with one million or more followers. They’re handpicked for major campaigns. Content creators fall on the other end of the spectrum, having anywhere from a couple hundred to more than 2,000 social media followers. Influencers fall somewhere in between, Callie Worthington, vp of brand marketing and creative at Shutterfly, told Digiday.

Shutterfly doesn’t have an internal pay scale that relates to its codification system. It is expected, however, that bigger creators (which could be tastemakers and influencers) command bigger budgets.

Rothy’s shoe brand also uses the term tastemakers. However, Rothy’s creator classifications are defined less by follower count and more by their cultural relevance, said Anna Doré, vp of brand and communications at Rothy’s. A tastemaker could be a TikTok star or a Substack writer. In comparison, Rothy’s refers to affiliate creators as “the lower funnel crater and conversion types,” Doré said.

Meanwhile, agencies don’t seem to go by the same guidelines. For example, VML organizes creators by their role in a campaign — salespeople, brand ambassadors, product collaborators, etc. The taxonomy isn’t as strict, Noah Eisemann, global executive director of creator and influencer at VML, told Digiday.

“Every influencer is a creator, but not every creator is an influencer,” Eisemann said.

The creator economy has exploded to where creators aren’t just selling products on behalf of brands. They’ve been hired as actors in Super Bowl spots. They’ve been brought on as product collaborators. Some are acting as creative agencies helping brands ideate and execute creative campaigns. Kim Kardashian is as much a content creator as she is an influencer, depending on what the brand asks of her, he added.

“The more useful standard may be a shared way of defining the role a creator is playing in a particular strategy — because that’s ultimately what should determine how we value the partnership and what success looks like,” Eisemann said.

A brand’s tiering setup influences how creators are evaluated, how their impact is measured and how they’re utilized in campaigns.

“When expectations, budgets, and measurement rely on different assumptions, effective work can be undervalued, and investment becomes harder to justify or sustain,” said Stefani Stamatiou, CEO of Americas at HYDP creator marketing agency.

But across the industry, there’s no conventional naming system. (Even Digiday staffers couldn’t agree on what defines a creator versus influencer.) And as more money pours into the creator economy, marketers are expecting measurement and business outcomes.

“Greater clarity around inputs and expected outcomes would make it easier to set goals, allocate budgets, and define the role creators play within the broader advertising landscape,” Stamatiou said.

Why the Daily Mail’s ad sales team is turning talent agent.

The company is increasingly leveraging partnerships with major sports leagues, college athletics and high-profile athletic events.

OpenAI, Google, Amazon and Walmart are betting on agent-led holiday shopping. Digiday charts how far it still has to go.

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This year’s Work/Life Awards finalists reflect a landscape where companies are rethinking workplace culture as a driver of business performance, employee engagement and innovation. #7433. Some creators are turning YouTube back catalogs into sizable paychecks

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Creators are turning their back catalogs into fresh revenue as platforms like Tubi hunt for more content to meet audience demand.

One true-crime creator, who requested anonymity, earned $154,000 across Spotify, Tubi, and Facebook this past September — more than 1.8 times what their channel made in the YouTube Partner Program that month, according to Chanel Simmons, md of digital media partner Motion Society, who did not provide specific YouTube figures.

That kind of return is part of a broader push to make existing content work harder. Motion Society has been syndicating creator content since 2022, but more creators and platforms are approaching them to help syndicate content now than ever. It works with over 650 creators and IP owners to distribute more than 30,000 videos a month across over 20 different digital platforms and TVs.

For Reign Maker Group CEO and co-founder Jonathan Chanti, the opportunity reflects a shift in creator strategy. “The focus is shifting from audience growth to maximizing the value of the content creators already own. Creators have spent the last decade learning how to produce content at scale. The next challenge is learning how to distribute and monetize it at scale,” he said. “Syndication is the natural evolution.”

Syndication is becoming increasingly popular in the creator space and Motion Society isn’t the only company investing in it. Lauren Lyster, vp and head of social media at Go Fish Digital, noted that agencies and managers can help individual creators syndicate. She pointed out that companies like Jellysmack have worked with creators like MrBeast and Brad Mondo, and Mirage Digital’s focus is on helping creators turn their catalogs into episodic content.

“Syndication is kind of like the new MCN [multi-channel network], but rebuilt around distribution instead of YouTube ad sales. The big bonus is that the destinations now pay. So the real value from the syndicators is operational,” said Keith Bendes, chief strategy officer at Linqia.

Kudzi Chikumbu, vp of creator partnerships at Tubi, said its audiences are always looking for authentic creator content. “This is why we look for creators whose body of work has a compelling, distinctive voice and aligns with Tubi’s fandoms, such as horror, YA, and reality,” he said. Tubi licenses content from creators like Joey Graceffa, who makes cinematic murder mystery episodes, and Kinigra Deon, who produces scripted drama and comedy series. Chikumbu said Tubi looks to partner with creators who have built something people are already invested in, which offers monetization in the form of revenue share.

“These platforms need a steady flow of programming, and creator content can help fill that need while bringing a built-in fan base with it,” said Lyster.

For creators, syndication can open additional revenue streams, but adapting and placing a catalog across platforms takes time and operational work. Companies like Motion Society handle that process, typically through revenue-share deals.

It starts its creator syndication process with curation, selecting and sequencing videos from catalogs into cohesive seasons. Then, the company re-edits the content to TV standards, which involves cutting out sponsor reads and social calls to action, removing watermarks, and editing out anything that doesn’t meet TV brand safety guidelines. The company will also cut out YouTube-specific content like mentions of previous videos or calls to follow or burned-in captions. Motion Society designers then make original key art for each show and season in platform-specific formats, and even A/B test strategies across platforms. All of this work saves creators the time and effort of adapting their catalogs for each platform. Motion Society typically takes a revenue share though it declined to disclose details.

These kinds of syndication partnerships give creators another way to monetize videos they’ve already made. As platforms seek more programming, distributors are betting there’s room to place more creator catalogs.

“Free streaming TV services are especially well positioned [to grab creators’ libraries]. Tubi has been openly building programs for creators, and services like Roku, Samsung TV Plus and Pluto TV also carry a lot of digital-first content. These platforms need a steady flow of programming, and creator content can help fill that need while bringing a built-in fan base with it,” said Lyster. She found Spotify an “interesting fit” for creators who do podcast-style video, and thinks Facebook works best for long-form content.

Syndication at this scale could result in shifting terms for brand partnerships. “Who owns the extra reach?” asked Bendes. “If brands are sponsoring the creator’s content and it gets syndicated, does that sponsorship carry? Usage rights, exclusivity windows and brand-safety clauses will need to catch up.”

Bendes expects sponsorship pricing for syndicated creators to move towards TV-style negotiation, with “a premium for multi-surface reach, and a lot of pushback from brands asking for proof that the reach is real.”

Lyster wondered if creators will start to fold syndication into how they run their business and where they post content and potentially follow a model more similar to how musicians use a distributor to get their music onto every streaming service.

Both Chanti and Lyster also noted that creator control could become increasingly more crucial if syndication takes off.

“The biggest risks are creators sacrificing control over their IP, audience relationships or future monetization opportunities for relatively modest short-term gains. Ownership, transparency and favorable licensing terms are critical,” Chanti said.

Grand Theft Auto 6 has its own content creator cottage industry, with millions of views and a crowded, competitive marketplace.

ChatGPT’s new AI visibility and click data can help inform publishers’ GEO strategies and complement third-party analytics.

More brands are gravitating toward live shopping, a growing channel that is set to play an outsized influence on shopping trends.

#7428. Future of TV Briefing: Everyone wants a clearer CTV bidstream, but who’s required to provide it?

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This Future of TV Briefing covers the latest in streaming and TV for Digiday+ members and is distributed over email every Wednesday at 10 a.m. ET. More from the series →

This week’s Future of TV Briefing looks at the latest call-to-action for the CTV advertising industry to tackle its transparency problem.

You may have heard that connected TV advertising has a transparency problem. So what’s anyone gonna do about it?

Well, the Coalition for Innovative Media Measurement has some ideas. Having surveyed 50 programmatic marketers about CTV’s signals situation, the industry organization has come up with a couple of recommendations.

First, companies need to agree to a minimum viable set of trading signals. The study’s proposed list of baseline signals includes standard parameters like IP address (as questionable a signal as it is), video placement and supply chain object (or “schain”).

These are already pretty standard signals. According to FreeWheel, one of the CIMM study’s sponsors, 95% to 100% of U.S. CTV and set-top box bid requests through its platform in February 2026 contained IP address, video placement and schain. However, other proposed baseline signals were slightly less common. For example, auction type was missing from 5% to 20% of FreeWheel’s sampled bid requests but would be table stakes under CIMM’s proposed signal set.

This gets at the underlying issue. Ad tech intermediaries don’t always support the signals that buyers and sellers want to be passing back and forth. Per the CIMM study, only 8% of the surveyed programmatic marketers — 4 out of 50 — said the demand-side platforms they use “always” are able to handle the buyers’ required signals.

To be fair, 58% of buyers said their required signals are “often” able to be handled by DSPs. But “often” is not “always,” which is the problem.

“The biggest asks that I got were from buyers and sellers that we want to understand which signals are making it all the way through the path and are actually operationalized versus used for reporting or just unusable,” said Emily Palmer, an industry consultant and chair of CIMM’s programmatic working group, who was one of the study’s authors.

Agreeing to a set of baseline signals would be one way to address this, though that would mean companies actually adhering to that agreement.

FreeWheel already requires certain signals like IP address in order to validate traffic, said Alex Strickland, head of programmatic supply relationships at the Comcast-owned ad tech provider that manages demand-side and supply-side platforms. But asked if FreeWheel requires all of the signals listed in the proposed trading baseline set, he said “we don’t require them.”

OpenX, the study’s other sponsor, takes signals into account when selecting which publishers’ bid requests to send to which DSPs, said Danner Close, vp of operations at OpenX. “We constantly engage publishers with, ‘Hey, here’s what is missing from a high-fidelity standpoint that’s preventing your request from going further or from garnering greater bid density’,” he said.

Supporting the table-stakes signals is one thing. Knowing which signals — beyond the baseline requirements (or would-be requirements) — an ad tech firm supports is another. This is where the CIMM study’s other recommendation is meant to come in. Ad tech firms would be asked to publish capability statements declaring which signals they support.

“Buyers and sellers would love to have a centralized resource [containing these capability statements]. That would make a big difference. That sounds somewhat simple; it sounds like it would be a work-in-progress; it sounds like each platform would have a product person occasionally update it; it would have to be an ongoing effort to be accurate and useful,” said Palmer. “The devil’s in the detail.”

Asked if FreeWheel already offers a form of capability statement, “I don’t think we have anything outside of our internal documentation that our customers have access to on our hub,” Strickland said.

For OpenX’s part, Close said, “We have external-facing docs for publishers that give them our general capabilities.”

Both FreeWheel’s Strickland and an OpenX spokesperson said their respective companies are committed to participating in that centralized resource containing the capability statements, though. Eventually.

“We are all actively, with OpenX as well, working to build a more efficient and competitive marketplace where our premium video content can be recognized and monetized appropriately, and we are willing to do whatever it takes to make that happen,” said Strickland.

Maybe that work will start soon. CIMM is looking to organize a voluntary pilot program for companies to adopt the proposed baseline signals set and implement capability statements. When will that pilot start, though? TBD.

This article has been updated to include Close’s response when asked if OpenX currently provides a form of capability statement.

“If we’re going to pay LinkedIn’s usual premium pricing, we’d want more clarity on how LinkedIn plans to accurately target their users on these devices.”

Behind that post is a back-and-forth over budgets, briefs, usage rights, and exclusivity —and the brand and the creator don’t always want the same things. During a session at Digiday’s recent Creator Strategies virtual event, YSL Beauty’s Hadley Musinsky and Night’s Sophie Bressler took the deal model apart step by step, from the brand’s first brief to the final post, and show how each side handles talent selection, creative alignment, and negotiation. #7427. Why Qualcomm teamed with F1’s new star in race for sports marketing ROI

The company’s consumer brand Snapdragon signed 20-year-old phenom Kimi Antonelli as a brand ambassador as it looks to connect with racing’s growing audience.

Snapdragon, the consumer brand of chipmaker Qualcomm, on Tuesday launched an anthem film around its partnership with Formula 1 driver Kimi Antonelli. The 20-year-old driver for the Mercedes-AMG Petronas team — of which the brand is also a partner — became the youngest Formula 1 World Champion ever earlier this month.

The video is part of a campaign timed to the 2026 Las Vegas Grand Prix on Nov. 21. It will run on F1 broadcasts outside the U.S. on Sky, on digital and in partnership with channels including Sports Illustrated and YouTube. Apple TV is the exclusive broadcast partner of F1 in the U.S., which prevents brands like Snapdragon from running traditional broadcast ads around the race.

“We've got other ways to bring it to life,” Qualcomm CMO Don McGuire said of the campaign. “We've got creators involved, we've got remixing involved, we have slipstreaming involved… hopefully turning it into an anthem for him that has really long legs.”

A 60-second hero video follows Antonelli during a day at a hotel. Wearing smart glasses and using a tablet, both powered by Snapdragon chips, the phenom listens to a version of the traditional Neapolitan song “Funiculì, Funiculà,” trains in the gym and kicks off a social media trend. The ad ends with a tagline, “Jamme, Jamme. To the top!,” which quotes the song and speaks to his meteoric rise in racing.

“We've been watching Kimi for quite some time, and we have always believed that he was meant for greatness and that he was going to ascend very quickly. I think we made the right bet,” McGuire said.

Snapdragon’s partnership with Antonelli builds on the brand’s deal with Mercedes-AMG Petronas, a strategic collaboration that kicked off in 2023. Qualcomm had previously partnered with the team in the 2010s, a deal that was not “ROI-driven, marketing-driven,” said McGuire, who became CMO in 2021. The new partnership looks to better connect the brand’s objectives with the growing audience of F1, both in the U.S. and globally.

“The sport more and more is not something that I can judge based on [advertising value equivalency]. We have some pretty good placements on the Mercedes cars, especially with cockpit cameras and things like that, but it's just so fleeting, from a visibility standpoint,” McGuire said.

Those issues are exacerbated by the Apple TV deal, which limits brands’ ability to measure impact during broadcasts. Qualcomm is committed to F1, Mercedes-AMG Petronas and Antonelli, especially as it seeks to bring the partnership from content and storytelling through activations and hospitality.

“We believe we're already seeing higher ROI coming out of some things that we've done in the short term,” McGuire said. “We believe in the sport, why we were there and the fundamentals.”

Outside of F1, Snapdragon is no stranger to sports. Since the start of the 2024-25 season, the brand has been the principal shirt sponsor of Manchester United. Despite the iconic soccer team’s on- and off-field issues in recent years, the deal has yielded “humongous ROI” for Snapdragon, McGuire said.

“When you're dealing with a sports brand that big, at scale, the brand can withstand the ebbs and flows of performance on the pitch because the fan base is so huge,” the executive explained. “That's the benchmark that we use [for sports partnerships] and maybe it's a little unfair because they are arguably the biggest sports brand in the world.”

Snapdragon’s latest F1 marketing comes on the heels of a holiday campaign that launched earlier this month. At the heart of “The Gift That Changes Everything” is a sweet 60-second hero spot that shows how a mom and dad make Christmas seem to come sooner. The parents use a Snapdragon-powered PC to learn how to build a "time machine" that gives their child a special Christmas morning experience.

The campaign is running across broadcast, connected TV and paid social in the U.S. with CTV and paid social in the U.K., Germany and France. Created in partnership with 72andSunny New York and 72andSunny Toronto, the effort iterates on a “Snapdragon. That’s How” creative platform that launched earlier this year. The campaign has seen strong preliminary results, McGuire reported.

The holiday creative arrives as the brand prepares for a season that could see hardware makers impacted by memory shortages, reducing Snapdragon’s total addressable market at a time when the Nvidia scandal is roiling the chip market. The brand continues to work to build awareness and affinity, regardless of short-term market conditions.

“This is about building muscle and emotion that can sustain the long-term,” McGuire said. “We feel really strongly that we had to lean into holiday as if it were any other holiday season, regardless of the macro conditions.”
